# Progress

- Added local `ingredient_usage` table (PK household_id+ingredient_name, FK to households) in `src/db/schema.ts`, generated `0004_ingredient_usage.sql` with drizzle-kit, added the table to `resetDb` in `src/test/db.ts`. Tests in `src/db/__tests__/ingredient-usage.test.ts`: insert and read back with defaults, duplicate PK rejected, same key allowed in two households, FK enforced.
- Added `src/features/lists/usage.ts` with `recordUsage` (upsert: count+1, new display name and time, clears hidden_at) and `unrecordUsage` (count-1 floored at 0, row deleted at 0); no callers yet. Tests in `src/features/lists/__tests__/usage.test.ts`: first use count 1, second use increments and updates name/time, per-household counts, decrement, delete at 1, missing-row no-op, count 0 never negative, hidden row unhidden by new use, rollback with the surrounding transaction.
- `applyAdd` in `src/features/lists/items.ts` now calls `recordUsage` (typed name, now) for every add, so `addItem` and `addItemsBulk` (and through it `addRecipeToList`, no extra call there) count one use per contribution: new, combine, reopen, repeats folded within a batch, and a recipe's "Keep existing" link. A kept-existing manual add or empty text counts nothing. `undoAdd` calls `unrecordUsage` for the item's household and key. Tests: "ingredient history" block in `items.test.ts` (new use with name/time/household, combine updates name and time, reopen, empty text, undo of new/combine/reopen, bulk counts, keep existing) and in `add-to-list.test.ts` (each chosen recipe ingredient +1 exactly once, including combined and kept-existing). No existing tests changed.
- `addRecipeIngredient` (insert now wrapped in a transaction with the usage write) and `addParsedRecipeIngredients` in `src/features/recipes/recipes.ts` call `recordUsage` once per created ingredient (typed name, recipe's household). `addItemsToRecipe` counts through `addParsedRecipeIngredients`; `updateRecipeIngredient` doesn't count. The counting rule is a comment at the top of `usage.ts`. Tests: "ingredient history" block in `recipes.test.ts` (recipe-only ingredient count 1 with household/name/time, a repeated ingredient counts 2, empty text and edit/delete/undelete don't count, parsed ingredients count once each, missing recipe throws and counts nothing, recipe-only ingredient goes to 2 when the recipe is added to a list) and in `from-list.test.ts` (Make recipe takes list items from 1 to 2 and adding that recipe to a list makes 3; Add to recipe doesn't count an ingredient the recipe already has). No existing tests changed.
- Added `backfillUsage()` to `src/features/lists/usage.ts`, called in `AppDataGate` right after `loadCatalogSeed` and guarded by its own device setting `usage.backfilled`. In one transaction it counts each live contribution (joined to its list item, deleted items included) and each live recipe ingredient per household and key; `last_used_at` and `display_name` come from the latest `created_at`, empty keys are skipped, and inserts use `onConflictDoNothing` so existing rows are never overwritten. Tests in the `backfillUsage` block of `usage.test.ts`: exact rows built from seeded lists/recipes across two households (deleted item counted, deleted contribution and deleted recipe ingredient not counted, latest text wins); running twice changes nothing; uses recorded after the backfill aren't counted again; an existing row (including `hidden_at`) is kept even with the device setting cleared; an add undone through `addItem`/`undoAdd` isn't counted; empty DB is a no-op. No existing tests changed.
- Added pure `src/domain/suggestion-rank.ts`: `frecency(row, now)` (`use_count * 0.5^(days/30)`; a future `last_used_at` counts as now) and `rankHistory(rows, query, now)` (trimmed, case-insensitive; matches when the display name or key starts with the query or has a whitespace-separated word that does; hidden rows dropped; sorted by score, then prefix over word match, then shorter display name, then alphabetical; empty query returns []). It's generic over the row type, so it returns the caller's own rows. Tests in `src/domain/__tests__/suggestion-rank.test.ts`: frecency values (half-life, future time, count 0); a frequent old item beats a once-used recent one, and loses to it after more decay; word match (`thighs`/`thi`); no match in the middle of a word; prefix beats word at equal score (even when it's the longer name); a higher-scoring word match beats a prefix; length then alphabetical tiebreaks; key-only match; case and whitespace; hidden rows dropped; empty/blank query; no matches; input not mutated. No existing tests changed.
- Rewrote `searchRecentItems` in `src/features/lists/items.ts` to read the household's `ingredient_usage` rows (hidden rows skipped) pre-filtered in SQL by the existing `p%` / `% p%` LIKE patterns on both `display_name` and `ingredient_name`, ordered by `rankHistory` and cut to `limit` after ranking; it takes an optional `now` for tests and returns `{ key, displayName }` as before. `items.test.ts` had no existing `searchRecentItems` tests to update (the one existing suggestion test, in `categories.test.ts`, passes unchanged), so I added a `searchRecentItems (spec 4.2)` block: key and latest display name; 5 uses 10 days ago outrank 1 use yesterday; a deleted list item stays in history; an undone add leaves nothing; word-start but not mid-word matches; key-only match (`zaatar` for `za'atar`); prefix beats word match; hidden rows and other households excluded; limit applied after ranking; case and whitespace; empty and wildcard-only queries; `%`/`_` stripped rather than treated as wildcards. No existing tests changed.
- `getSuggestions` in `src/features/lists/suggestions.ts` is now history-first: `Suggestion` gained `source: 'history' | 'catalog'`; with 2+ shown history matches only history is returned (up to `limit`), otherwise `searchCatalog` fills the remaining slots, deduped by key. A suggestion equal to the typed name is still skipped and so doesn't count toward the 2 (my reading of "history returns 2 or more matches"). `applySuggestion` now takes `Pick<Suggestion, 'key' | 'displayName'>` so callers without a source still type-check. Tests in new `src/features/lists/__tests__/suggestions.test.ts`: `chi` with chicken thighs/breasts in history returns exactly those two (no chicken salad); history-only capped at limit; one history match then catalog fills the rest, and a history key also in the catalog appears once as history; no history equals the catalog results; `chickp` drops history and shows chickpeas; `2 lbs chi` equals `chi`; exact-typed name skipped and not counted; hidden rows and other households aren't history; short text returns []; `applySuggestion` keeps the amount for both sources. Existing `categories.test.ts` suggestion test passes unchanged.

- Households migration. Wrote `supabase/migrations/20261009120000_households.sql` by hand (the `supabase` CLI needs approval in this session, so `supabase migration new` couldn't run): `households` (with `created_at`/`updated_at` and the `set_updated_at` trigger), `household_members` (role check owner/member, index on `user_id`), `private.my_household_ids()` (stable security definer, execute granted to `authenticated` only, plus usage on schema `private` so policies can call it), RLS with select-only grants and member-only select policies, and `profiles_select_own` replaced by `profiles_select_own_or_household`. pgTAP test `supabase/tests/02_households.test.sql` (18 assertions: Alex sees the shared household and both member rows but not Pat's; reads Sam's profile but not Pat's; direct insert/update/delete rejected; role check; Pat sees only own members; anon gets permission denied on both tables and the function). Migration file written by hand (the CLI was unavailable to the loop at the time). Verified by hand on 2026-10-09: `supabase db reset` applies both migrations and `supabase test db` passes (2 files, 27 tests).
- Added `supabase/migrations/20261009181124_create_household.sql` (via `supabase migration new`): `public.create_household(p_id, p_name)` (security definer, execute for `authenticated` only; raises 42501 with no user, 22023 for a null id or blank/null name; inserts the household with `on conflict do nothing`, then if it already existed returns for a member and raises 42501 for anyone else; adds the caller as owner at their max position + 1) and `public.get_household_members(p_household_id)` (stable security definer, `authenticated` only; raises 42501 for non-members and unknown ids; joins profiles, ordered owners first, then display name, then user id). New pgTAP file `supabase/tests/03_create_household.test.sql` (28 assertions): create stores the row with `created_by` and makes the caller owner at position 1; second household gets 2; Pat with positions 1 and 3.5 gets 4.5; repeat calls (same or different name) change nothing; another user with the same id raises and neither joins nor renames; empty/whitespace/null name and null id raise and create nothing; no user in the token raises; `get_household_members` returns exact rows (ids, names, avatar, role, joined_at) in owner-then-name order, a solo household returns one row, a member of another household, a non-member and an unknown id raise; anon can't call either function. `supabase db reset` and `supabase test db` pass (3 files, 55 tests); jest, lint and typecheck pass. No existing tests changed.

- Households screens. Settings shows a "Households" section, with one `SettingsLinkRow` per local household from `householdsQuery` (live). `SettingsLinkRow` is a new tappable row variant with a chevron in `src/components/settings-group.tsx`. - Auto-increment: `summarizeAmounts` (`src/domain/quantity-math.ts`) now counts an amount with no quantity as `1` with no unit when there are two or more amounts, so it sums with unitless counts (`milk` twice → `2`, `[2, null]` → `3`, `[1 gal, null]` → `1 gal + 1`); a lone null still reads `''`. Stored quantities stay null. `itemTotal` is unchanged (it still drops recipe links with no quantity). `src/domain/recipe-grouping.ts` called `summarizeAmounts` directly, so it would have started counting links (a recipe added twice for `salt` → `2`; a deleted recipe's link under Other items → `+ 1`). It now applies the same link filter as `itemTotal`, and its contribution type gained `source`. Callers checked: the combine toast says "now 2"; `getItemEdit` shows `2` and saving it unchanged leaves the contributions alone; "Make recipe" from `milk` added twice now gives the recipe ingredient quantity 2 (was null). Changed existing tests: in `quantity-math.test.ts`, "shows nothing…" no longer expects `[null, null]` → `''` (it's now in the new counting test as `2`); in `recipe-grouping.test.ts`, fixture `c` gained a `source` field (defaults to recipe/manual; no assertions changed). New tests: `quantity-math.test.ts` (`[null,null]`→2, three nulls→3, `[2,null]`/`[null,2]`→3, `[0.5,null]`→1½, `2 can + 1`, `2.5 lb + 1`, `1 gal + 2`, `4 + 1 dozen`); `recipe-grouping.test.ts` (manual nulls count under Other items, a recipe link with no quantity isn't counted in a recipe section, a twice-added recipe null stays `''`, a deleted recipe's link isn't counted); `items.test.ts` "adds with no amount count as one" block (toast `2`/`3`, `2 avocados` + `avocados` → 3, stored quantities stay null, edit sheet `2` and an unchanged save keeps the contributions, `2 lb` + Keep existing link → `2 lb`, manual `milk` + link → `''`); `from-list.test.ts` (make recipe from milk ×2 → quantity 2). jest (294), lint and typecheck pass.
- Added `ingredient_unit_usage` to `src/db/schema.ts`: `household_id` (FK households), `ingredient_name`, `unit_key`, `use_count` (default 0), `last_used_at` and the shared timestamps. The primary key is `(household_id, ingredient_name, unit_key)`, with `// TODO(M5): sync` above the table. Also added the `IngredientUnitUsage` type. Generated `src/db/migrations/0005_ingredient_unit_usage.sql` (plus the journal, snapshot and `migrations.js`) with `yarn drizzle-kit generate --name ingredient_unit_usage`, and added the table to `resetDb`. New pure `src/domain/usual-unit.ts`: `unitKey` returns `'count'` for null. `unitFromKey` is the inverse and returns undefined for unknown keys. `usualUnit` picks the highest count, then the latest `lastUsedAt`, then the alphabetically first key. It returns null for count and undefined when nothing is known (no rows, all zero, or only unknown keys). It skips rows with a zero count or an unknown key. Tests: new `src/db/__tests__/ingredient-unit-usage.test.ts` covers insert and read back with defaults, the primary key rejecting a duplicate triple, two units allowed for one ingredient, the same triple allowed in two households, and the FK check. New `src/domain/__tests__/usual-unit.test.ts` covers key mapping and a round-trip of every unit, unknown keys, and undefined for empty, all-zero and only-unknown rows. It also covers a single unit, count → null, the highest count beating a more recent unit, a tie broken by recency, and a full tie broken alphabetically (including `count` vs `lb`). It also covers an unknown key with the highest count ignored, zero-count rows ignored, and input order not mattering and not being mutated. No existing tests changed. Nothing under `supabase/` changed. jest (311), lint and typecheck pass.
- Record units with usage: `recordUsage`/`unrecordUsage` in `src/features/lists/usage.ts` take an optional `amount: Amount`. With a quantity, `recordUsage` upserts the `ingredient_unit_usage` row for `unitKey(amount.unit)` (`use_count + 1`, `last_used_at = at`), and `unrecordUsage` decrements it (never below 0) and deletes it at 0. No quantity (or no amount) writes nothing. Callers pass the amount: `applyAdd` passes the contribution (a Keep existing link has quantity null, so it records no unit), `addRecipeIngredient` passes the parsed `{ quantity, unit }`, `addParsedRecipeIngredients` passes each item's amount, and `undoAdd` reads the undone contribution's quantity/unit and passes that. Updated the usage-counting comment at the top of `usage.ts` and `applyAdd`'s doc comment. New "unit usage" block in `usage.test.ts` (13 tests): direct record (lb, bare number → `count`); no amount / null quantity writes no row; `2 lb` → `1 lb` → `3` gives `lb` ×2 @T2 and `count` ×1 @T3; `milk` twice writes no row; undo decrements then deletes at 0; undoing an add with no amount leaves units alone; undo of a new item and of a reopen; unrecord on a missing row and on a 0 count; Keep existing records a use but no unit; recipe `400 g chickpeas` records `g`, and adding the recipe to a list makes it 2 with the newer time; recipe `salt` records none; `addParsedRecipeIngredients` records `cup`/`count` and skips a null quantity; another household's rows are untouched. No existing tests changed. Nothing under `supabase/` changed. jest (324), lint and typecheck pass.
- Backfill units: new one-time `backfillUnitUsage()` in `src/features/lists/usage.ts`, guarded by device setting `usage.unitsBackfilled` and run in `AppDataGate` right after `backfillUsage`. A household+ingredient pair that already has unit rows is skipped. New `backfillUnitUsage` block in `usage.test.ts` (7 tests): exact rows from seeded lists and recipes in two households (gal ×2, count, lb from a deleted item plus a recipe, can, l); a deleted contribution, a deleted recipe ingredient and no-quantity adds aren't counted; `ingredient_usage` is untouched; running it twice changes nothing; units recorded after it aren't counted again; an ingredient with unit rows already is kept as is, even with the device setting cleared, while the same ingredient in another household is still backfilled; it runs regardless of `usage.backfilled`; an empty DB is a no-op. No existing tests changed. Nothing under `supabase/` changed. jest (331), lint and typecheck pass.
- Usual unit in totals: `summarizeAmounts(amounts, usualUnit = null)` counts a null quantity as 1 of `usualUnit` (unitless when null), and `itemTotal(contributions, usualUnit = null)` passes it through. Not changed: `from-list.ts` ("Make recipe" still parses `itemTotal` with no usual unit, so `milk` ×2 with usual gal gives a recipe quantity of 2, not 2 gal). - Amount stepping data functions: `src/domain/quantity-math.ts` gets `stepAmount(amount, direction, usualUnit = null)` (step 1 for count/package units, oz, fl oz, pt, qt, gal; 0.5 for lb, kg, l, tsp, tbsp; 0.25 for cup; 100 for g, ml; the result is rounded to the step and never goes below one step; − at or below one step → null; a null quantity is 1 of `amount.unit ?? usualUnit`) and `combineAmounts(amounts, usualUnit = null)` (the one amount a total comes to, or null; empty → 1 of the usual unit). `formatGroup` now formats through a new private `combineGroup`, and grouping moved into a private `groupAmounts`, so `summarizeAmounts` output is unchanged. In `items.ts`: `steppableAmount(contributions, usualUnit)` drops recipe links with no quantity (through a new `countsInTotal`, shared with `itemTotal`) and then calls `combineAmounts`. `setItemAmount(itemId, amount)` and `stepItemAmount(itemId, direction, usualUnit)` follow; `stepItemAmount` returns the new amount, or null with nothing written when the total isn't one amount or − is already at the minimum. Both replace the counted contributions in one transaction through a new `replaceAmounts` helper pulled out of `updateItem` (a single contribution is updated in place; several are soft-deleted and replaced by one manual amount). They leave recipe links with no quantity alone. `updateItem` behaves as before: it still replaces all its contributions, links included. Interpretation: I read "replace the item's manual amounts" as every contribution that counts toward the total, including a recipe amount that has a quantity, so the total equals the new amount. New tests: `quantity-math.test.ts` `stepAmount` (every unit stepped up and down; the usual unit ignored when there's a quantity; − at or below one step → null; down to exactly one step; off-grid amounts clamped; stepping up from below one step; float rounding, including `0.25 cup` stepped up 3 times = exactly 1 and `0.1 + 0.2` cup; a null quantity with gal, lb and no usual unit; input not mutated) and `combineAmounts` (single, same unit, `2 lb + 8 oz` → `2.5 lb`, mixed → null, usual unit, empty). `items.test.ts` "changing an amount" block: `steppableAmount` (single, same-unit sum, convertible, mixed → null, nothing → usual 1 or count 1, links ignored); `stepItemAmount` (in place on a single amount; several replaced by one manual amount; `1 gal milk` + `milk` → `3 gal`; a lone `milk` with and without a usual unit; − at the minimum and mixed units change nothing; a recipe link is kept and still shows in By recipe; a missing item throws); `setItemAmount` (replaces `milk` ×2 with `1 gal`; in place for one amount; keeps a link, and adds an amount beside a lone link; a missing item throws). - Edit sheet stepper and unit picker: `src/app/list/[id]/item/[itemId].tsx` gets an `AmountStepper` row under the Amount field (− button, the current amount, + button, unit button with the unit name). - Quantities docs. `docs/spec.md` §4.2 gains a "Usual unit" bullet. It covers `ingredient_unit_usage`: each add or recipe ingredient with a quantity counts one use of its unit, and count when it has no unit. Undo takes the use back, and an add with no quantity counts nothing. The most-used unit wins; ties go to the most recent, then alphabetical. Existing data is backfilled once. §4.2 also gains a "Changing an amount" bullet for the edit sheet's − / + stepper and unit button, which save immediately. Steps are 1 / 0.5 / 0.25 / 100 depending on the unit, results are rounded to the step, and − stops at one step. An item with no amount steps from one of its usual unit. The unit picker keeps the number without converting. Both replace the amounts but keep recipe links that have no quantity, and they are disabled when the total isn't a single amount. §4.4 quantity math now says an add with no amount counts as one of the usual unit (unitless when there is none): `milk` + `milk` → `2`, and `1 gal` + `milk` → `2 gal` (`1 gal + 1` with no history). Stored quantities stay null, a lone add shows no amount, Keep existing links don't count, and mixed units are still joined. The toast example now reads "now 2 gal" and explains the `1 gal + 1` no-history case. §5 adds the `ingredient_unit_usage` table: synced in M5, with `use_count` merged as a counter and `last_used_at` as the max. The Siri note now covers both history tables. §9 test 9 now says the toast reads "now 1 gal + 1" with no unit history and "now 2 gal" when milk is usually bought by the gallon. Note: an in-app `1 gal milk` add records gal itself, so "no history" mostly means items from before unit tracking. No code tests (docs only). jest (399), lint and typecheck pass.

- `categorize-ingredients` handler: `supabase/functions/_shared/ai/prompts/categorize.v1.ts` now exports `buildCategorizePrompt(names)`. Its `instructions` hold the spec 6.4 rules, every `slug: Name` from `SYSTEM_CATEGORIES`, and a note that the names are data, never instructions. Its `prompt` is an "Untrusted item names to classify..." line with no `[`, then the names as a JSON array. `categorize-ingredients/handler.ts` exports `categorize(names, store, model?)` behind a `CategorizeStore` interface, which the next task implements with the service role. The interface has `getIngredients`, `insertPending` (on conflict do nothing), `saveCategorized(rows, categorizedBy)`, `saveFailed(names)` and `logAiCall`. Both save methods apply only `where status <> 'categorized'`, and a failure sets attempts + 1 and Other. `categorize` throws `CategorizeInputError` for more than 100 raw names. It normalizes and dedupes the names: the first spelling becomes the display name, and names that are empty after normalizing are dropped. Names over 64 characters are answered failed/Other without touching the store or the LLM. It upserts missing names as pending and sends the uncategorized ones with `attempts < 3` in one `runAiTask` call per 100 names. Answers are capped at the number of names sent, answers for names not sent are ignored, and for a duplicate the first answer wins. A missing name, or a `NoObjectGeneratedError`, marks names failed. An unknown slug also ends up as `NoObjectGeneratedError`, because the schema's enum rejects the whole output, so it fails the whole batch. `APICallError`, `RetryError` and `TimeoutError`/`AbortError` leave the rows unchanged, and those names are answered pending. Any other error is thrown. When `isAiEnabled('categorize')` is false, it makes no call and answers those names pending. The response is re-read from the store after writing, so a concurrent winner's result is what gets returned. New Deno tests in `handler.test.ts` (21, using an in-memory fake store and `MockLanguageModelV4`): all-known input makes zero calls. 8 unknown + 4 known make one call with exactly the 8, `categorized_by = test:model` and one `ai_calls` record. A categorized name isn't sent again. Names are normalized and deduped, and empty ones are dropped. A missing name becomes failed/Other with attempts 1. An unknown slug fails the whole batch (`invalid_output`). A schema failure increments attempts (0→1, 1→2). An answer for a name not sent is ignored, and for a duplicate answer the first wins. Answers past the number sent are ignored. A provider error and a timeout each leave the batch pending with attempts unchanged (a failed row stays failed in the DB but is answered pending). An unexpected error is thrown. `off` and unset both make no call and answer pending. A 65-character name is failed/Other and is neither sent nor stored, while a 64-character name is sent. A name at attempts 3 isn't sent, while one at attempts 2 is. 101 names are rejected with nothing written, and 100 make one call. A name with injected instructions appears only in the prompt's JSON array, never in the instructions or the preamble. `buildCategorizePrompt`'s instructions don't change with the names. A row categorized concurrently isn't overwritten. `yarn dlx deno test --allow-env --allow-read --allow-sys` (34 pass). No existing tests changed. jest (426), lint, typecheck, `supabase db reset` and `supabase test db` (97) pass.

- `categorize-ingredients` endpoint (task now says option (a)): `index.ts` wraps `withSupabase({ auth: 'user' })` (so the library sends 401 before our code runs) and calls the pure `handleRequest(req, { userId, categorize, env? })` in `request.ts`, with `userId` from `ctx.userClaims!.id` (not used yet; the rate limit task keys on it) and a store from `createCategorizeStore(ctx.supabaseAdmin)` in `store.ts`. `handleRequest` checks in this order. Anything but POST is 405. Then an unset, empty or blank `AI_MODEL_CATEGORIZE` is 500 `not_configured` ("AI_MODEL_CATEGORIZE is not set"); `off` counts as set, and the kill switch still answers pending. A `content-length` over 16 KB is 413 without reading the body, as is a body that turns out over 16 KB while it's read (capped streaming read). Bad JSON, `names` not an array of strings, or more than 100 names is 400 `invalid_request`, as is a `CategorizeInputError`. Any other error is 500 `internal_error` with a generic message, and only the error's message is logged, never headers or the token. In `store.ts`, `ingredients` is global, so there's no user filter. `getIngredients` uses `in`. `insertPending` is an upsert with `ignoreDuplicates`. `saveCategorized` makes one update per (category, is_grocery_item) group, with `neq('status','categorized')`. PostgREST can't express `attempts + 1`, so `saveFailed` reads the rows and writes `attempts + 1`, status failed and Other back, guarded by `eq('attempts', old)` and `neq('status','categorized')`, so a concurrent increment isn't counted twice. `logAiCall` inserts into `ai_calls`. - Per-user rate limit (spec 6.4), done after the task was reworded to settle the earlier blocker. Migration `20261009230000_categorize_limit.sql`: `private.categorize_usage` (PK user_id+day, FK to auth.users on delete cascade) and `private.categorize_daily_total`, with RLS on and all grants revoked from public/anon/authenticated. `public.check_categorize_limit(p_user_id, p_new_names, p_daily_cap) returns text` is `security definer`, `search_path = ''`, and only service_role can execute it. Days are UTC and the minute is a fixed `date_trunc('minute')` window. It upserts a zero row, then locks the user row and then the day's total `for update`. Counters change only on 'ok'; a refused first request leaves a zero row. A negative or null argument raises 22023. A cron job (`categorize-usage-retention`) deletes old rows. Edge Function: `CategorizeStore.checkLimit(newNames)` calls the RPC. `categorize` calls it once per request, after reading the DB and before writing anything, with the LLM-bound count (0 when AI is off). On a refusal it throws `CategorizeLimitError(reason)`, so a refused request writes no pending rows. `handleRequest` maps `user_limit` to 429 `rate_limited` and `global_cap` to 503 `daily_cap_reached` with `Retry-After: 3600`. `index.ts` builds the store with the user id and `dailyNameCap(CATEGORIZE_DAILY_NAME_CAP)` (default 20,000; invalid values fall back to it). Documented in `supabase/functions/.env.example`. Tests: new pgTAP `07_categorize_limit.test.sql` (35): 60 a minute, then refused even with 0 names; another user unaffected; the window resets; 500/501 names a day, and a batch that crosses the limit is refused whole; refused requests leave counters unchanged; the global cap refuses every user, but 0 new names still gets ok; a null user skips the per-user limits but not the cap and writes no usage row; bad args raise; anon/authenticated can't call the function or read the tables, and service_role can call it. Deno, 10 new (76 pass): checkLimit counts only LLM-bound names; all-known counts 0; off counts 0; user_limit/global_cap refuse with no LLM call and no writes; a check error propagates; 429; 503 + Retry-After; 400 comes before the limit; `dailyNameCap` parsing. The existing FakeStore got a `checkLimit` method; no existing assertions changed. jest (426), lint, tsc, `supabase db reset` and `supabase test db` (132) pass. Not checked by hand against the served function: the RPC call in `store.ts` has no automated test.

- Categorize on add: `src/features/categories/categorization.ts` adds `categorizePending(names?)`. With names, it makes one `functions.invoke('categorize-ingredients', { body: { names } })` with exactly those names. With no argument, it's the catch-up run: up to 100 local `pending`/`failed` names (`catchUpNames()`, sorted by name), sharing one in-flight promise. Per-add calls aren't coalesced. It does nothing for an empty list, missing Supabase config or no session. It never throws: a network error, an error response (429/503) or a response with no `results` warns and leaves rows as they are. `applyResults` writes in one transaction: `pending` answers are skipped; `categorized` sets category (Other if null), status and `is_grocery_item`; `failed` goes to Other. It updates only rows not already `categorized` locally, ignores names with no local row, and never touches overrides. DEVIATION: the function's response has no `categorized_by`, so the client writes the marker `'server'` (`CATEGORIZED_BY_SERVER`) for categorized rows and null for failed ones. It isn't `'seed'`, so the seed loader won't overwrite those rows, and sync (M5) will bring down the real model id. Added `categorization.test.ts` (22): explicit names sent exactly, in one request; an empty list makes no request; per-add calls aren't coalesced; results written with exact values; overrides untouched; failed → Other; categorized with a null category → Other; a pending answer leaves the row pending; a failed answer doesn't undo a locally categorized row; answers for non-local names are ignored; a bad category id rolls back the whole batch; no session or no config means no request; a network error, an error response and missing results leave rows pending without throwing; catch-up sends pending and failed names but not categorized ones; nothing pending means no request; the 100-name cap; two concurrent catch-ups share one promise and one request; a new catch-up can start after the previous one finishes; per-add calls run alongside a catch-up. jest 448, lint and tsc pass. Not called from the app yet (that's the next task).
- Call categorization from the app: `ensureLocalIngredient` now returns true when it inserted a pending row, and each add returns the names it newly marked pending. `AddItemResult` has `pendingNames` (addItem and each addItemsBulk result); `updateItem`, `updateRecipeIngredient`, `addParsedRecipeIngredients`, `addItemsToRecipe` and `addRecipeToList` return `string[]`; `addRecipeIngredient` returns `{ id, pendingNames } | null`; `makeRecipeFromItems` returns `{ recipeId, pendingNames }`. New pure `collectPendingNames(results)` in `categories.ts` dedupes a bulk add's names in order, so a recipe or bulk add makes one call. Callers fire `void categorizePending(names)` without awaiting: the list add bar, the item edit sheet (rename), the recipe add bar and ingredient edit, "Add to list" from a recipe, "Make recipe" and "Add to recipe". "Newly marked" means a row was inserted, so a name already pending or failed isn't re-sent on each add (catch-up covers it), and adding a recipe whose ingredients were already sent from the recipe screen sends nothing. New `src/features/categories/catch-up.ts`: pure `isOnline` (connected, and reachability not false), `cameOnline(was, now)` (only false → true; the first state isn't a reconnect), `cameToForeground`, and `startCatchUpTriggers(onCatchUp)` using `AppState.addEventListener('change')` and expo-network's `addNetworkStateListener`. That's the SDK 57 API, read from the installed package's types because WebFetch wasn't allowed. `AppDataGate` runs `categorizePending()` in an effect keyed on userId (at launch, after the useMemo's `ensureMissingIngredients`, and again on sign-in) and starts the triggers once. Added `expo-network` with `yarn expo install` (a native module, so it needs a new dev build). DEVIATION: there's no Siri queue drain yet (only the M0 spike screen; the real drain is M8), so there was nothing to wire there; `addItem(..., 'siri')` already returns `pendingNames` for it. Tests: new `pending-names.test.ts` (21): collectPendingNames order, dedupe, nulls and empty input; addItem with an unknown name → [name], a seed name → [], combine and reopen → [], an already-failed name → [], empty text → null; bulk add names per result and collected, a known-only bulk → []; updateItem renamed to an unknown, known or already-pending name, and edits with no rename; addRecipeIngredient id and names, a known name, a repeat, empty text; addParsedRecipeIngredients returns only new names, deduped; updateRecipeIngredient; acceptance 6 (8 names the catalog lacks → all 8 in one array); a recipe already sent → []; a kept-existing conflict still records its name; Make recipe and Add to recipe. New `catch-up.test.ts` (12): isOnline, cameOnline, cameToForeground, and the triggers (no call on start, one per return to the foreground, a launch in the background, the first network state ignored, offline → online fires once, unreachable → reachable, cleanup removes both listeners). CHANGED EXISTING TESTS (return shapes only, no assertions changed): `recipes.test.ts` and `categories.test.ts` read `addRecipeIngredient(...)!.id`, and `from-list.test.ts` reads `.recipeId` from `makeRecipeFromItems`. jest (481), lint and tsc pass; `supabase/` not touched. NEEDS A MANUAL CHECK in a new dev build, with the function served on the mock model. `za'atar` is in the seed catalog, so it never shows Sorting...; use an unknown name like `flux capacitor` (the mock puts it in pantry) instead. For the 8-ingredient recipe, the names are sent when they're typed into the recipe, so adding that recipe to a list sends none; check one request and one LLM call for the recipe's names. Airplane mode: add an unknown item, turn the network back on, and see it sorted.

- Pantry tokens, fonts and DESIGN.md: installed `@expo-google-fonts/bricolage-grotesque` and `@expo-google-fonts/figtree` with `yarn expo install`. They're embedded at build time by the `expo-font` config plugin in app.json: Bricolage 500–800 and Figtree 400–700, with iOS file paths and Android `fontFamily`/`fontDefinitions` objects. I set it up from the installed plugin's types and source because WebFetch isn't allowed. The fonts are referenced as `'Bricolage Grotesque'` and `'Figtree'` with `fontWeight` choosing the file. Those are the typographic family names read from the TTF name tables, and the same strings are registered on Android. Native code changed, so this needs a new dev build. `theme.ts`: the Pantry light palette with the task's values (`controlBorder`, `inkTertiary`, `primarySoft`/`onPrimarySoft`, `dangerSoft`, `pending`/`pendingSoft`, `scrim`, `toast`, plus `onToast` #FFFFFF for the toast text). A derived dark palette (ground #111915, cards #1B241F, primary #7CC395). `AisleColors` (dot and soft per hue, light and dark), exposed as `useTheme().aisles`. `Fonts` is now `{ display, body }`. Typography: largeTitle 40, screenTitle 36, sheetTitle 30 and title 28 in display 800 with −0.025 em tracking; sectionTitle display 17/700; headline 17/700, body 17/500, callout 16/500, amount 15/600, sectionLabel 13/700, footnote 13/400 and caption 12/700 in Figtree. Rounded gets card 22, tile 22, sheet 28 and row 16; I kept lg and xl because Button still uses xl. The top comment is fixed, and global.css's web font variables now point at the new faces. Removed the unused `highlight`/`success`/`warning`/`info` tokens. `item-row`'s add flash now uses `pendingSoft` instead of highlight; the list task will make it the aisle tint the boards show. The undo toast now uses `toast`/`onToast`. New pure `src/domain/aisle-color.ts` `aisleColor(slug)` and `src/domain/contrast.ts` (`relativeLuminance`, `contrastRatio`). DEVIATION: the brief doesn't map `deli`, so I gave it rose (meat and cheese counters), and household/pet → green, personal-care/baby → blue. `inkTertiary` #7C877F is only 3.7:1 on white, so DESIGN.md and the theme limit it to icons and decorative glyphs (tested at ≥3:1). Wrote DESIGN.md: the direction, fonts, every typography/colour/aisle token with its use, the aisle colour rule, the radii, and the card, row with round check, section header, pill, segmented control, CTA, soft destructive button, badge, add bar, toast, sheet, tile and nav-button patterns. `yarn dlx expo-doctor`: 21/21 checks pass. Tests: `aisle-color.test.ts` (every seed slug has a hue, only `other` is grey among seeds, all 20 exact mappings, unknown/empty/case/`constructor`/`__proto__` slugs and null/undefined → grey). `contrast.test.ts` (black/white luminance, channel weights, lower case, bad formats throw, 21:1, symmetry, #777/#767 vs white). `src/constants/__tests__/theme.test.ts` (exact light palette and aisle values; both schemes have the same keys and #RRGGBB values; 16 text pairs ≥4.5:1 in each scheme; ink and inkSecondary on every aisle soft; the Owner badge pair; inkTertiary ≥3:1; display vs Figtree per variant; exact sizes, weights and tracking; only embedded weights; line height ≥ size; radii). Lists are grouped under their household name (`sectionLabel`) as a two-column grid of `tile` (132 pt minimum) tiles: the count in the new `tileCount` typography (display 34/800), or "All done", above the name. The Siri default list (`getDefaultListId`, live on `userSettings`) is filled `primary` with a small mic "Siri" mark. A dashed "New list" tile ends each household's grid. Edit and + stay native in the toolbar; reorder mode, the context menu and archived lists (now tiles under the Archived toggle) are unchanged. `listsWithCountsQuery` now also returns `householdId` and `householdName` (inner join on households). **List screen**: the native title is gone. The content header has the `screenTitle` name with "N to get" beside it, then the `SegmentedControl`, now "By aisle" / "By recipe" (stored value still `category`). Each aisle is a `Card` with a `SectionHeader` (aisle dot from `aisleColor(slug)`, count). The Sorting… section is a header-less `pendingSoft` card with dashed amber checks and an amber "Sorting…" label (`sectionLabel`) on each row. Each row's VoiceOver label ends with the section's old label ("Sorting, finding a section for these items"). By recipe: a card per recipe with a 36 pt `IconTile` (new `src/components/icon-tile.tsx`, `fork.knife`) in `recipeHue(recipeId)` and a round actions button (`iconWell`, 44 pt hit slop) that opens the old action sheet. Other items has neither. Shared items get "Also in <recipe>". Checked items keep the Checked (N) toggle and are in their own card. `ItemRow` takes the card's ground, a hairline above every row but the first, and `amount` text; checked rows get the filled check and `inkTertiary` struck-through text. The toast now reads "Added to **milk**, now 1 gal + 1" (`Toast` got an `emphasis` prop). The merged row stays tinted with its aisle's soft colour for as long as the toast shows (replacing the 1.2 s timer). **Add bar**: suggestions are a floating `surface` panel (in flow above the input, since iOS drops touches outside a parent's bounds) with a "YOU USUALLY BUY" caption ("SUGGESTIONS" when only the catalog matched), an aisle dot, the typed part in bold, and the short aisle name on the right (new `ingredientAisles` in categories.ts: the user's override, else the default). This also applies on the recipe screen, which shares `ItemAddBar`. New pure helpers: `src/domain/text-match.ts` `matchParts`; `aisle-color.ts` `recipeHue` (hash over green/blue/rose/amber; the recipes task can reuse it) and `shortAisleName`; `contrast.ts` `mix`; `src/features/lists/list-view.ts` (`groupListsByHousehold`, `pairs`, `tileCount`, `tileAccessibilityLabel`, `toGetLabel`, `alsoInHint`, `itemRowLabel`, `addedToast`, `SORTING_LABEL`); `appearance.ts` `listTileColors`, `siriMarkColors`, `itemNameColor`. Siri/archived; toGetLabel; alsoInHint; itemRowLabel incl. Sorting; addedToast with and without a total). Added to `aisle-color.test.ts` (recipeHue exact values, empty id, stable, all four hues and never grey, long ids; shortAisleName cases) and `contrast.test.ts` (`mix` at 0/1/0.5/0.12, clamping, bad formats). `appearance.test.ts`: tile colours and their contrast, Siri mark values and ≥4.5:1 over the blended wash in both schemes, itemNameColor, the new sizes. `lists.test.ts`: household fields and grouping with a second household. `categories.test.ts`: `ingredientAisles` defaults, per-user override, signed out, custom aisle (no slug), pending/unknown left out, empty. `suggestions.test.ts`: `suggestionCaption`. - DONE (2026-10-10) "Server: `apply_mutations`": The sync decision is still open in `docs/spikes.md` §3, but this task holds either way: the spike says PowerSync's `uploadData` would also call `apply_mutations`. **The PowerSync vs custom decision still needs making before the client tasks (HLC/outbox onward).** New migration `20261010120000_apply_mutations.sql`. `public.apply_mutations(mutations jsonb)` is `security definer` and executable by authenticated only. It returns one result per mutation, in order: `applied`, `unchanged`, `merged` (with `merged_into`) or `rejected` with SQLSTATE `code` and `message`. Each mutation runs in its own subtransaction, so one rejection (e.g. 42501, not a member) doesn't block the rest; the push task can use that to record and skip. It does field-level LWW by HLC against `field_clock` and delete wins (deleted_at is its own field, an undo is a newer null). Re-sends are no-ops. The 7.3 merge works like this: a would-be second open item is soft-deleted with the new `list_items.merged_into` set, its contributions move to the open item, later contributions follow `merged_into`, and reopening a merged item merges it again. `updated_at` stays server-set and `created_at` is taken on insert only. How I read ambiguities (all documented in spec §5, 7.1-7.3): the HLC wire format is `'<13-digit ms>-<4 hex counter>-<device id>'`, compared as text. The usage tables name the row with `key` instead of `id`, and `use_count` goes as `counters` deltas, applied once per row + hlc via `private.applied_counters` (spec 5 says use_count merges as a counter). I added the server `ingredient_unit_usage` table (counter plus max `last_used_at`, with a `field_clock` kept for uniformity), since it is synced household data that didn't exist on the server yet. `field_clock` already existed on the other household tables. Direct insert/update/delete on all seven household tables is revoked from authenticated; the old `for all` policies are replaced by select-only ones. Not covered yet: `households` (still changed via its own functions), the per-user tables (overrides, order, settings: still direct writes, so the push task needs to decide whether they also go through `apply_mutations`), and usage rows that reach use_count 0 (they now stay on the server, since direct delete is revoked, so the client should treat ≤0 as absent). Side effect: the dev "Push local data to Supabase" (`dev-push.ts`) can no longer write household tables on a real server. The Membership task deletes it anyway. Tests: new `supabase/tests/08_apply_mutations.test.sql` (35 checks: insert with clocks and device created_at, newer wins/older ignored per field, HLC tie-breaks by counter and device, concurrent check + note both kept, delete wins over a newer edit, older undo ignored and newer undo clears deleted_at, a re-sent batch/insert is unchanged, counters add once per hlc, unit last_used_at max, duplicate open item merged with its contribution re-pointed and re-merge on reopen, non-member edit/insert rejected without blocking the batch, invalid mutations rejected with codes (unknown table, bad hlc, server-owned fields, household move, unknown field, bad counter, missing id, missing column), non-array batch raises, direct writes revoked, anon can't call it). I changed the existing `05_household_data.test.sql` because the task changes what it checks: its direct inserts now go through `apply_mutations`, "second open item is rejected (23505)" became "list items can't be inserted directly" (the merge is covered in 08), and "usage rows can be deleted" became "can't be deleted directly". `supabase db reset` and `supabase test db` pass (167). jest 909/909, lint and tsc pass.
- DONE (2026-10-10) "Client: HLC and outbox": **Built on the custom sync design, which the M5 section of tasks.md assumes. `docs/spikes.md` §3 still says the PowerSync vs custom decision is open. If you pick PowerSync, this task and the M5 tasks after it need rewriting.** New `src/sync/`. `hlc.ts` is pure: `formatHlc`/`parseHlc` in the spec 7.1 wire format, `compareHlc` (text order), `tickHlc` (monotonic even when the wall clock goes back; a full counter carries into the next ms) and `receiveHlc` (adopts a larger remote clock). `clock.ts` keeps the device id (a UUIDv7) and the last HLC in device setting `sync.clock`, written in the caller's transaction. It has `nextHlc`, `observeHlc` (for the pull task; ignores malformed and older HLCs) and `getDeviceId`. `mutation.ts` holds the wire `Mutation` type. `mutate.ts` is the one write helper: `insertRow`, `updateRows`, `upsertRow`, `deleteRows`. Each runs in a savepoint inside the caller's transaction and writes the row, its local `field_clock` (json_patch of field to HLC) and one outbox row per affected row. An insert sends every column except the key, counters and `updated_at`/`field_clock`. An update sends exactly the patched fields. It refuses keys, counters, server-owned fields, SQL values and non-synced tables. Counters go out as `counters` deltas and change locally by the delta, floored at 0. Schema: drizzle-kit migration `0006_sync_outbox.sql` adds `field_clock` to every household and per-user table, plus `outbox` (autoincrement id, `household_id` (null for per-user rows), `mutation` json, `created_at`). Every write in lists.ts, items.ts, usage.ts, recipes.ts, from-list.ts and categories.ts (custom categories, order, overrides) now goes through these helpers. `setCategoryOverride`/`clearCategoryOverride` take an optional `tx` so `updateItem` keeps them in its transaction. How I read ambiguities (documented in spec 7.1 "Client side"): per-user tables and `households` aren't in `apply_mutations`, so their mutations use the same shape and the push task decides how to send them. Hard deletes of overrides and order rows send `delete: true`. A usage row at 0 is deleted locally only, and its decrement goes out as a counter. The backfills now enqueue their usage rows (`use_count` as a counter). Custom categories are enqueued as per-user data. Left as direct local writes on purpose: the catalog (`ingredients`, system categories), device settings, and the recipes list's `touch` (a local `updated_at` sort bump, since `updated_at` is server-set). `dev-push.ts`'s `toServerRow` now leaves out `field_clock`. Tests added: `src/sync/__tests__/hlc.test.ts` (format widths, round-trip, malformed text rejected, out-of-range parts refused, ordering by time/counter/device including hex counters, tick monotonic under a backwards clock, counter carry, flooring, 500-tick strictly increasing, receive adopts larger/keeps own/compares counters, tick after receive beats the remote). `src/sync/__tests__/mutate.test.ts` (insert fields and clock, outbox household id, update sends only patched fields per row and moves only those clocks, no-op update writes nothing, refused patches, composite usage keys with counters, counter-only update leaves the clock alone and floors at 0, per-user upsert/delete with null household, a failed insert leaves neither row nor mutation, a later throw in the caller's transaction rolls back row, mutation and clock, increasing HLCs with the device id, `observeHlc` adopts larger and ignores older or malformed, stable device id). `src/sync/__tests__/write-paths.test.ts` (exact outbox output of first run, list create/rename/archive/delete/move/default, add new/Combine/reopen, check/uncheck/delete/undelete, undo, checkItems/clearChecked, item edit with and without a category override, duplicate rename and unreadable amount roll back with no mutation, setItemAmount, removeRecipeFromList, recipe create/edit/delete with unlinking, recipe ingredient add/edit/delete/undelete, addItemsToRecipe links, hideFromSuggestions, custom category with order rows and move, override set/reset), plus the `src/test/outbox.ts` helpers. One existing test changed, because the row gained a column: `categorization.test.ts` "leaves the user's overrides alone" now expects `fieldClock: {}` on the untouched override row. The check is otherwise the same. `supabase/` not touched. jest 965/965, lint and tsc pass.
- DONE (2026-10-10) "Push": new `src/sync/push.ts`, built on stash's `uploadPending` + `backoff.ts` (same 5 s / 30 s / 2 min / 10 min / hourly schedule, same coalescing as stash's `syncNow`: a call during a run shares its promise and runs once more). `pushNow({ force?, now? })` drains pending outbox rows in `id` order. Consecutive household-table mutations go to `apply_mutations` in batches of 100. Each result is handled on its own: `applied`/`unchanged`/`merged` remove the row, and `rejected` records `rejected_at` + `error` ({ code, message }) on the row, which is then skipped for good. Those two columns are new on `outbox` (drizzle-kit `0007_outbox_rejections.sql`). Any call-level failure (network/thrown, status 0, 401, 408, 429, 5xx, or a malformed response) stops the run with the queue untouched and backs off. The backoff state (`attempts`, `nextAttemptAt`, `lastError`, `lastSuccessAt`) lives in device setting `sync.push` for 7.5. Automatic pushes wait out the delay; a forced one (launch, sign-in, foreground, reconnect) doesn't. How I read an ambiguity (spec 7.1 left it to push): `apply_mutations` doesn't take per-user tables or `households`, so they go one at a time in their place in the queue. Per-user rows are direct upserts/deletes limited to the granted columns. Categories are an insert of id/name/owner (a 23505 re-send counts as done) or a rename. A household insert goes to `create_household`, and any other household change is recorded as `unsupported`. A non-retryable 4xx there is recorded and skipped too. Triggers: `mutate.ts` calls `notifyOutboxWrite()` (new `outbox-signal.ts`, which keeps network code out of `mutate.ts`). After `startPush()` that debounces a push 1 s. `startCatchUpTriggers` got an optional `onNetwork(online)` callback so push skips while offline and pushes on reconnect and foreground. There's also a retry timer for the backoff delay. `AppDataGate` calls `startPush()` and pushes on sign-in. `QueryResult` in the fake Supabase gained `status`. Known gaps for later tasks: data written before the outbox existed (task "Client: HLC and outbox") has no mutations, so it isn't pushed. `registerHouseholds` still runs alongside (harmless, since `create_household` is idempotent; the Membership task deletes it). An override for an ingredient the server catalog lacks will be rejected (FK 23503) and recorded. Tests: new `src/sync/__tests__/push.test.ts` (20): the backoff schedule; order kept in one batch with the exact mutations and state; batches of 100/50 in order; per-user and household mutations interleaved in queue order (create_household → apply → user_settings upsert → apply); merged/unchanged count as sent; per-user upsert/delete/category insert and rename payloads; a duplicate category insert counts as done; a rejected mutation recorded with code/message/time and never re-sent while later writes go; a per-user 4xx recorded while the queue moves on; a household rename recorded as unsupported; retry with backoff (failure keeps the queue, 5 s then 30 s, waits until due, then sends in the original order and resets); force ignores backoff; thrown/401/malformed responses retry rather than drop; per-user 401/500 hold the queue behind them; the retry timer fires at 5 s; offline holds and reconnect sends; signed out/unconfigured send nothing; debounce sends a burst as one batch; foreground pushes; a push requested during a run runs once more. `catch-up.test.ts`: `onNetwork` hears every report, before the reconnect catch-up. Spec 7.1 says how push sends per-user and household mutations. No existing tests changed; `supabase/` not touched. jest 986/986, lint and tsc pass.
- DONE (2026-10-10) "Pull with per-household cursors": new `src/sync/pull.ts`, wired into `AppDataGate` (`startPull()` on launch, foreground and reconnect, skipped while offline, plus `pullNow()` on sign-in; calls coalesce like the push's). A run reads the user's `household_members` rows. It then pulls each household's tables, parents first (households, lists, recipes, list_items, recipe_ingredients, contributions, ingredient_usage, ingredient_unit_usage), then the user's own tables (categories, order, overrides, settings). Then `ensureMissingIngredients()`, so pulled names are cached as pending, and then `ingredients`. Cursors live in device setting `sync.pull`, keyed `household:<id>:<table>`, `user:<table>` and `catalog:ingredients`. Each is the server's `updated_at` text, so microseconds survive. A different user resets them. How I read ambiguities: "per-household cursor" is per household *and* per table, because one cursor for several tables read one after another can skip rows. Paging is keyset on `updated_at`: a full page's last timestamp is fetched with `eq` so one `apply_mutations` batch is never split. Each table's first page reaches 5 s (`PULL_OVERLAP_MS`) behind its cursor, for transactions that commit late; re-applying is a no-op. A row SQLite refuses (FK: its parent arrived after its table was read) stops that table with the cursor before it, so it's retried rather than lost. LWW: a remote field wins when its HLC is >= the local `field_clock` (equal takes the server's, which covers the server's merges and re-points that keep the clock). With no server clock (per-user tables, households) it wins unless an unpushed outbox mutation sets that field. An unpushed local delete keeps the row away. `use_count` = server total + unpushed deltas (<= 0 removes the row). Unit `last_used_at` takes the max. Pulled HLCs go through `observeHlc`. Combine on clash: a remote open item soft-deletes a different local open item for the same list + ingredient and takes its contributions, written directly and not to the outbox, since the server merges the same way when the local one is pushed. The catalog pull only updates names the device has (7.4) and never downgrades a categorized row. A first catalog pull pages through all server catalog changes once. Known gaps, noted in spec 7.2: per-user rows hard-deleted on another device aren't seen (Realtime delete events, next task). A `default_list_id` for a list not on this device is skipped. The local starter "Personal" household isn't reconciled with a server one created on another device (the Membership task moves creation to the server). Tests: new `src/sync/__tests__/pull.test.ts` (34). `remoteFieldWins` rules; first pull (table order, exact queries, rows with server timestamps and clocks, cursor per table, no outbox writes, pulled names cached as pending); incremental pull uses `gt` cursor − overlap; the cursor never moves back; separate cursors per household; joining pulls only the new household in full and leaves the others' cursors as they were; non-member households not pulled; a new user starts over; paging across a tie at the page end; LWW (newer remote wins with its clock, local unpushed note kept while remote check applies, older remote ignored after push, remote delete, server merge drops the local row and moves its contribution, local clock adopts pulled HLC); Combine on clash (remote add, remote reopen, other ingredients and checked items untouched, outbox unchanged); counters (server + pending delta, 0 removes, unit max); preferences (pulled with cursors, unpushed change kept and pushed one replaced, unpushed delete not brought back, unknown default list skipped); catalog (known names updated, unknown ignored, no downgrade); failures (FK stop and retry, cursor before the failing row, network error recorded with earlier cursors kept, then cleared; signed out does nothing; a request during a run runs once more). Spec 7.2 has a "Client side" note. No existing tests changed; `supabase/` not touched. jest 1020/1020, lint and tsc pass.
- DONE (2026-10-10) "Realtime": I chose "trigger a cursor pull per household", so pull stays the only path that applies remote rows. Server: new migration `20261010150000_realtime.sql` adds the 8 household tables, `household_members`, `categories`, the 3 per-user tables and `ingredients` to the `supabase_realtime` publication. Realtime then applies each subscriber's RLS select policies. Replica identity stays the default (primary key). Client: new `src/sync/realtime.ts`, `startRealtime(userId)`, started from `AppDataGate` while signed in. It opens one channel per household (each household table filtered `household_id=eq.<id>`, or `id=eq.` for `households`), a user channel (`household_members` and the user's 4 own tables, filtered to the user) and a catalog channel (`ingredients`). An event schedules a scoped pull. Events within `REALTIME_BATCH_MS` (300 ms) of the first share one pull. A household event pulls that household, a user-table event pulls the user's tables, and a catalog event pulls the catalog. A `household_members` event for this user does a full pull. Each full pull reports the memberships it read through the new `onMemberships`, and channels are opened and closed to match. Each channel pulls its scope once SUBSCRIBED, which covers the gap since the last pull and rejoins after errors. Background or offline closes every channel. Foreground reopens them, and a reconnect replaces them with new ones. An event that arrives on a channel after it has closed is ignored. `pull.ts` changes: `pullNow({ scope })` with `PullScope` (`households`, `user`, `catalog`). A scoped run doesn't read memberships. Coalescing now merges the scopes asked for during a run (`mergeScopes`), and a full request makes the next run full. I also added `applyRemoteDelete`, which fills the gap the pull task left: Realtime DELETE events for the user's `user_category_order`/`user_category_overrides` rows (hard-deleted, so pull can't see them) delete the local row from the event's key, unless the outbox has an unpushed change for it. Delete events can't be filtered, so other users' deletes arrive and are ignored, including other members' `household_members` deletes. The remote check animates like a local one with no UI change: the list's `useLiveQueryOn` re-runs on any SQLite change, and the row's Reanimated layout/entering animations don't care where the write came from. Known gaps for the Membership task: a lost membership only closes that household's channel. Its local rows and outbox entries stay until that task's purge. Household hard-deletes (cascades) just trigger a pull of that household. Spec 7.2 has a "Realtime" client note. Tests added: `src/sync/__tests__/realtime.test.ts` (20), which drives the real pull against a fake server: which channels open and their exact bindings/filters; channels before and after memberships are known; not configured opens none; cleanup closes all and cancels a scheduled pull; a remote check comes down within the batch window through the pull (only that household's 8 tables queried, local item checked); several events share one pull and two households pull both; another household's change isn't delivered; user-table and catalog events pull only those; a deleted override is applied without a pull while another user's delete is ignored; SUBSCRIBED catches up per channel; CHANNEL_ERROR just warns; joining a household does a full pull and opens its channel; losing one closes only that channel; another member's removal is ignored; background/foreground, starting in the background, offline/reconnect (new channels) and staying online (same channels); events on closed channels do nothing. In `pull.test.ts` (13 new): `mergeScopes`; household-, user- and catalog-scoped pulls query exactly those tables; an empty scope; scoped calls during a run merged into one more run; a full request upgrades the next run; `onMemberships` (each full pull, replay for the same user only, scoped pulls silent, unsubscribe, a failed lookup reports nothing); `applyRemoteDelete` (deletes without an outbox write, keeps a row with an unpushed change, ignores another user/missing row/incomplete key/other tables, order rows). Also new: `supabase/tests/09_realtime.test.sql` (exactly these tables published, no private tables, default replica identity). The fake Supabase gained Realtime channels (`channel`, `removeChannel`, `emitChange`, `setChannelStatus`, `openChannels`). No existing tests changed. `supabase db reset` and `supabase test db` pass (170), jest 1053/1053, lint and tsc pass. The two-simulator check is in manual-checks.md.

In one transaction it deletes the household's contributions, items, recipe ingredients, usage and unit usage, lists, recipes and the household row, plus all its outbox rows (rejected ones too). It clears a Siri `default_list_id` pointing into it locally, as the server's `on delete set null` does, without queuing a mutation. A full pull purges households that were on the previous list and aren't on this one, after the remaining households are pulled. It also drops their cursors, so joining again pulls in full. How I read "lost": only a household the server once listed for this user can be lost. So a household created here and not pushed yet is never purged. An empty list is treated as a bad answer (everyone has a household), so it purges nothing. Scoped (Realtime) pulls never purge. A new user starts with `memberships: null`. State saved before the field existed loads with null and only records the list. Subscribing and unsubscribing were already done by the Realtime task (full pull → `onMemberships` → channels). Household creation stays on the outbox (`ensureStarterData` → `insertRow` → push → `create_household`). `registerHouseholds` is deleted from `households.ts` and `AppDataGate`, and the Developer section is removed from Settings. A rejected mutation for a lost household is already recorded and skipped by push (drop, not retry), and the purge deletes it. Known gap: a second device signing in to the same account still makes its own starter "Personal" household (TODO on `ensureStarterData`). Spec: 7.2 has a "Client side" note on membership purges, and §10's M5 notes no longer mention `registerHouseholds`/dev-push. Tests: new `src/sync/__tests__/purge.test.ts` (10). It covers: every row of the household removed, with exact counts; the other household's rows, contributions, usage and outbox unchanged; queued and rejected mutations dropped while per-user ones stay; overrides and a default list elsewhere kept; a default list in it cleared with no new mutation; view mode, last-opened and registration settings for it cleared while others stay; a last-opened list elsewhere kept; an unknown household purges nothing; a later throw rolls the purge back; purging both households. `pull.test.ts` gained 9 "lost memberships" tests: lost household purged with its mutations and cursors while H1 and its queued rename are kept; rejoining pulls in full; a never-listed local household survives; an empty list purges nothing; a run that fails before the households are in doesn't purge but the next does; scoped pulls don't purge; older saved state only records; another user doesn't purge the last user's households; a default list in the lost household is cleared. `realtime.test.ts` gained 1: a `household_members` DELETE removes that household's data and keeps the other's. - DONE (2026-10-10) Safe page fetch and readable text. New `supabase/functions/_shared/fetch-page.ts`: `fetchPage(url, { resolve, fetch, timeoutMs })` returns `{ finalUrl, title (string | null), jsonLd, text }` or throws `FetchPageError` with a `reason` (`invalid_url`, `blocked_address`, `dns_failed`, `too_many_redirects`, `http_status`, `not_html`, `too_large`, `timeout`; import-recipe should map them all to `fetch_failed`). It allows http(s) only. It checks every hop with `redirect: 'manual'`: IP literals directly, `localhost`/`*.localhost` refused, other hosts through the injected resolver (default `Deno.resolveDns` A + AAAA), and refuses if any address is blocked. It follows at most 5 redirects. One 10 s timeout covers DNS, every hop and the body, and aborts and cancels them. The 3 MB cap is checked against Content-Length and while streaming. The content type must be exactly `text/html`; the body is decoded with the declared charset, falling back to UTF-8. `isBlockedAddress` covers IPv4 private, loopback, link-local (incl. 169.254.169.254), CGNAT (incl. 100.100.100.200), documentation, multicast and reserved ranges. For IPv6 it covers ::/96, ::1, ULA (incl. fd00:ec2::254), link-local and site-local, multicast, documentation and discard, and checks the IPv4 inside mapped, NAT64 and 6to4 addresses. Anything unparseable is blocked. `readPage(html)` is a pure regex/tag-depth reader with no DOM dependency. It takes the title and every `application/ld+json` block (commented-out ones skipped). For the text it prefers schema.org Recipe microdata or a recipe-plugin card (`recipe-card`, `wprm-recipe-container`, `tasty-recipes`, `mv-create-card` class), then `<article>`, then `<body>`, moving on if one has no text. It drops scripts, styles, comments, nav, header, footer and aside, including nested ones. Block tags become lines, other whitespace is collapsed, entities are decoded, and the text is capped at 30,000 chars without splitting a surrogate pair. My choices, not in the task: it also drops `head`, `noscript`, `template` and `svg`; it keeps line breaks at block elements instead of collapsing everything to one line, so ingredient lists stay readable; an unclosed removed element loses only its tag. Known gap (commented in the code): fetch re-resolves DNS after the check, so DNS rebinding isn't fully stopped. Pinning the address needs a custom HTTP client. Exported the entity decoder from `src/shared/recipe-jsonld.ts` as `decodeHtmlEntities` (fetch-page imports it by relative path because `_shared/` has no import map). Tests: new `supabase/functions/_shared/fetch-page.test.ts` (35 Deno tests, run from `_shared/` with `deno test --allow-read fetch-page.test.ts`, README updated). They cover blocked and allowed IPv4/IPv6 tables; literal private hosts incl. decimal/hex IPv4, bracketed v6 and localhost, never fetched; private or mixed DNS answers; redirects to private hosts never fetched; DNS re-checked per hop; 5 redirects ok, 6th refused; non-http redirect, missing Location; bad URLs; DNS failure; non-HTML types; case and charset; error statuses; the body cap via header (stream never read) and via stream (cap+1 refused, exactly 3 MB ok); timeouts on fetch, a stalled body (cancelled) and DNS; a fixture page (`_shared/fixtures/recipe-page.html`) with exact title, JSON-LD and text, and end to end; and the readPage preference, fallback, nesting, removal, whitespace, attribute, JSON-LD, title, text cap and surrogate cases. Also 2 jest tests for `decodeHtmlEntities` in `recipe-jsonld.test.ts`. jest 1291/1291 (baseline 1289), lint, tsc, `deno check`, `supabase db reset` and `supabase test db` (211) pass.

The instructions hold the rules from the task, the unit list and the output contract. The page goes only in the prompt, as JSON after an "untrusted" line with no `{` in it. Input caps: lines trimmed, blank ones dropped, then 80 lines of 300 chars; text 30,000 chars. In `tasks.ts`: the `extractRecipe` task (`AI_MODEL_RECIPE`, `AI_MODEL_RECIPE_FALLBACK`, temperature 0, `AI_THINKING_RECIPE` overriding `RECIPE_THINKING` = categorize's `low`, `recipeMaxOutputTokens` = 300 + 60 per line, capped at 6,000), plus `cleanExtractedRecipe`. `.env.example` has `AI_MODEL_RECIPE=google:gemini-3.8-flash`. Not in the task, my choices: (1) the model is held to a looser `ExtractRecipeOutputSchema` (no length caps, unit any string). With the strict `ExtractedRecipeSchema`, one long name or odd unit would fail the whole call, and "unknown unit → null, wording in note" and "long names dropped" couldn't happen. `cleanExtractedRecipe` applies the caps and returns output that passes `ExtractedRecipeSchema`: whitespace collapsed; names over 64 chars or that normalize to empty dropped; units resolved through the quantity parser's spellings (`cups`, `Tablespoons`, `fluid ounces`); an unsupported unit becomes null with its wording put at the front of the note; `count`/blank means no unit; name and notes cut to 120, the list to 80 (dropped entries don't count); a quantity or servings ≤ 0 becomes null. The `name` keeps the model's wording; import-recipe should normalize it for `ingredientName`. (2) A 300-char cap on the page title. (3) The builder also returns `itemCount` (lines sent, or 80 for page text). (4) `timeoutMs` is 30 s, pending real timings (docs/spikes.md §4 isn't measured yet). (5) `runAiTask` casts `task.output` to `z.ZodType<unknown>`, because TS can't type `Output.object` with a union of schemas; it's still validated, and the result cast was already there. `mock-model.ts` has a shared `mockModel` helper now, plus `mockExtractRecipe`/`pageFromPrompt`/`mockRecipeModel`. They run `parseQuantity` over each line (from text, the lines that start with an amount), drop headings that end in `:`, and put anything after the first comma in the note. Tests: new `supabase/functions/categorize-ingredients/extract-recipe.test.ts` (24 Deno tests; it lives there for the import map, like ai.test.ts). They cover: page content only in the prompt, instructions the same for every page; the rules and units in the instructions; injection and JSON-breakout lines staying data, also end to end through the mock; every input cap and surrogate truncation; output caps (name, notes, 80 entries, drops not counted); 64 vs 65 char names and empty-after-normalizing names; unknown units → null with note; unit spellings; positive quantity and servings; mock outputs; temperature, maxOutputTokens, thinking, instructions, prompt and no tools reaching the model; the `AI_THINKING_RECIPE` override; env and `isAiEnabled`; lenient model output cleaned; schema-invalid output logged as `invalid_output`. My choices, not in the task: (1) statuses: `fetch_failed` is 502; `invalid_url` is 400, also for `blocked_address` (fetchPage's `invalid_url`/`blocked_address` → 400, every other reason → 502); 503 `Retry-After` is 3600 for the cap and `off`, 30 for model failures; protocol errors reuse categorize's extra codes (`invalid_request`, `method_not_allowed`, `payload_too_large` at 4 MB, `not_configured`, `internal_error`). Updated spec 6.6's error line to match. (2) Invalid model output is 503 too, not cached; a non-AI error from `runAiTask` is a 500. (3) Every import counts against the limits, cache hits included (the check comes before the cache, as the task orders it). (4) A page with no JSON-LD recipe and no text is `not_a_recipe` with no LLM call (cached if server-fetched). (5) JSON-LD's `servings` wins over the model's; the model's name wins, falling back to the JSON-LD name or page title when it's empty. (6) If categorize's own limit refuses, the import still succeeds with every name `pending`; other categorize errors are 500. (7) A cache hit is served even when the client also sent a page. (8) A failed cache write is logged and the import still succeeds. - DONE (2026-10-11) Client import. New `src/features/recipes/import.ts`. `importRecipe({ url, listId, page?, importId? })` calls `import-recipe` and checks the response with `RecipeImportResponseSchema`. Then, in one SQLite transaction, it:
  - finds the household's live recipe whose `source_url` canonicalizes to the response's, or the one an earlier run of the same `importId` created;
  - otherwise creates one. The id is `recipeIdForImport(importId)` (UUIDv5 under `IMPORT_ID_NAMESPACE`), or `newId()` without an import id; if a deleted recipe already holds that id, it gets a fresh one. The name falls back to the host when the page gave none, and notes are `Serves N`. Ingredients (with notes) go in through `addParsedRecipeIngredients`, only when the recipe is new;
  - adds them all to the list through `addItemsBulk` (recipe contributions, Combine), then runs `applyResults` with the response's categories.

  It returns `{ ok: true, recipeId, recipeName, listId, added, combined, undo }`: `added` is the number of ingredients added, `combined` how many of them joined an open item. `undo` runs `undoAdds`, plus `deleteRecipe` if this import created the recipe, in one transaction. Error results:
  - `not_a_recipe`;
  - `offline`: no config, no session, a `FunctionsFetchError`, or invoke throwing;
  - `rate_limited`: 429, or `unavailable` (503); both read "Try again in a minute";
  - `failed`, with `reason: 'invalid_url' | 'fetch_failed'` when the server gave one, so the UI can say "Couldn't reach that site" or "That doesn't look like a link". A URL that isn't http(s) fails before any call.

  Decisions:
  - Usage is counted once per ingredient, by the list add (spec 4.10: "counts as a recipe add"). So `addParsedRecipeIngredients` got a `{ countUsage: false }` option, and undo puts usage back exactly.
  - In the app, a `db.transaction` inside another one fails: expo-sqlite's drizzle driver sends a second BEGIN, while the better-sqlite3 test DB nests without complaint. So `applyResults`, `addItemsBulk`, `undoAdds`, `addParsedRecipeIngredients` and `deleteRecipe` now take an optional `exec` (the caller's transaction, default `db`). Existing callers are unchanged.

  New tests in `src/features/recipes/__tests__/import.test.ts` (33):
  - new recipe: exact recipe and ingredient rows, list rows, and each item's recipe contribution; request body with and without a page; no name → host; no servings → null notes; one top-level transaction for the save and one for undo; usage counted once; the same ingredient twice folds into one item;
  - existing recipe by canonical URL (stored non-canonical) is reused and left untouched; recipes in another household and deleted ones are ignored;
  - an unchecked item Combines (exact contributions); a checked one is reopened with its amounts replaced;
  - categories land locally: failed → Other, nothing pending, a single invoke; a seeded ingredient keeps its category; a name the server left pending stays pending;
  - import ids: the id is the UUIDv5; the same import id twice → one recipe; re-importing after undo gets a new id;
  - undo restores the list, the checked state and usage, and deletes a new recipe but keeps an existing one;
  - every error case returns its typed result and writes nothing (checked against a snapshot of every table the import writes, outbox included): 422/429/503/502/400 bodies, a status with no JSON body, a fetch error, invoke throwing, a malformed response, an unknown list, a non-http URL, no session, no config.

  jest 1332/1332, lint and tsc pass. No `supabase/` changes.

- Drain shares in the app: added `src/features/recipes/share-drain.ts`. `drainShareQueue(userId, { pending, recordFailed })` imports `readPendingShares()` one at a time, oldest first, with one drain at a time. Each share goes to its envelope's list, or the default list (`siriTargetListId`, whose parameter type now also takes a share envelope), with the envelope's `page` and `importId = envelope.id`. Success deletes the file and clears its try count. `not_a_recipe` records a failed import and deletes the file at once. Other errors keep the file and count a try in device settings (`shareImportAttempts:<id>`, with a new `clearDeviceSetting` helper); the 5th try records it as failed and deletes it. Bad files are logged and deleted; with no list to import into, everything waits. One deviation to review: after `offline` or `rate_limited` the drain stops, so the shares behind it wait without counting a try (otherwise five offline launches would fail the whole queue). I added a sentence saying so to spec 4.10. `startShareDrain` runs on launch and sign-in (AppDataGate, next to the Siri drain) and on foreground and reconnect (`startCatchUpTriggers`). `onShareImports` feeds the list screen, which shows the import toast with Undo and View recipe for one share, or "Added 3 recipes" (`sharedImportsToast` in import-sheet.ts) for several. The `failed_imports` table doesn't exist yet, so `recordFailed` defaults to a log line; the next task should make its table writer the default. Tests: new `src/features/recipes/__tests__/share-drain.test.ts` (22 tests): happy path (exact recipe, list rows, file gone); page and import id sent; oldest first; default list for null, unknown, deleted and archived lists; offline, rate_limited and failed keep the file and count tries; offline stops the drain; other failures move on; the 5th failure records `{ url, title, reason }` and deletes; success clears the count; not_a_recipe fails at once with a null title; bad files deleted and logged; `.tmp` skipped; two concurrent drains import once (I confirmed this test fails without the guard); a crash between import and delete gives one recipe; no list → waits; signed out → waits; listeners get one call per drain; no App Group → null; Undo; toast copy. jest 1450/1450, lint and tsc pass. No `supabase/` changes. `.scratch/main.swift` is still there; delete it before committing.

- BLOCKED, not checked off: "Make the Add to recipe sheet show its content". The task's first step is to reproduce the bug in the iOS simulator and write down what shows and why before changing anything. I can't do that here, and WebFetch was denied, so I couldn't read the SDK 57 formSheet docs either. Without either one, the cause isn't clear from the code. What I ruled out by reading it: the `items` param is fine. `addToRecipe` (`list/[id]/index.tsx:297`) pushes `[...selection].join(',')` (UUIDs), and Cancel only renders after the `!list || itemIds.length === 0` early return, which returns a bare `<Screen />` with no toolbar. The one layout difference from the item sheet that works (`list/[id]/item/[itemId].tsx`, same `[0.6, 1]` detents) is the structure: that sheet uses `<Screen scroll>`, so its ScrollView is the screen's root view. `add-to-recipe.tsx` puts a `ScrollView` with `flex: 1` and `contentInsetAdjustmentBehavior="automatic"` inside a plain `flex: 1` View. But `import-recipe.tsx`, which the task calls a working sheet, has exactly that structure too (plus a footer View below the ScrollView, at `[0.5, 1]`), and its own manual check is still open. So the structure alone doesn't explain it, and changing it now would be a guess when the task says to fix the cause. Next step needs someone at the simulator: open the sheet from Select mode and check whether the content is at zero height (view hierarchy debugger), hidden behind the header, or missing, and confirm `import-recipe` really renders. A likely first thing to try is switching `add-to-recipe.tsx` to `<Screen scroll …>` like the item sheet. Baseline jest 1475/1475 passed. No code changed. `.scratch/main.swift` is still there; delete it before committing.
